        Idea:
# Sprint 8: Orchestrated Route Propagation and Lifecycle Contract

## Overall Context

This sprint brings parent/child workflows onto the selector contract. It is the point where travel, join, and edit-video orchestration must reject mixed or unsupported backend combinations before creating partial work.

## Shared Operating Rules

- Parent backend selection is authoritative only when all required child routes are supported, tested, and not WGP-only.
- Child rows must carry route snapshot metadata: selected backend, selector version, parent route key, and support state.
- Preserve dependency arrays, idempotency, cancellation, duplicate completion handling, and repair paths.
- Fail closed before child creation when backend consistency cannot be guaranteed.

## Sprint Goal

Route parent/child workflows consistently through selected backend where Comfy support exists.

## Required Deliverables

- Propagation for travel/join/edit-video parent and child surfaces.
- Persisted child-row route snapshot.
- Dependency-array/idempotency/cancellation behavior for DB-created child rows.
- Parent/child backend-consistency guards.
- Lifecycle-contract tests.
- Repair/runbook hooks for partial children, uploaded-but-not-completed outputs, duplicate completion, mixed-backend child sets, and parent repair.

## Exit Criteria

Parent rejects Comfy if any required child route is WGP-only, unsupported, fallback, or untested; child rows created after parent claim carry enough metadata to avoid selector drift; lifecycle contract is green for every USED route intended for canary.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Sprint 8 Route Propagation And Lifecycle Contract

## Overview
The relevant implementation is split across `reigh-app` and `reigh-worker`. API task creation already stamps a nested `params.route_contract` via `reigh-app/supabase/functions/create-task/routeContract.ts`, and DB claim filtering reads that contract in `20260506050000_route_contract_claim_gate.sql`. Worker-created child tasks already flow through `add_task_to_db()` and can pass route snapshot fields, with join children partially covered in `reigh-worker/tests/test_template_routing.py`.

The simplest fix is not a new orchestration system. Keep `params.route_contract` as the persisted contract, make parent route validation explicit for orchestrated families, and add one worker-side child route propagation helper that all travel/join/edit-video child creation paths use. Fail closed before any child rows are inserted when the parent selected backend cannot be honored by every required child route.

## Phase 1: Contract Foundation — Shared Route Semantics

### Step 1: Extend the route contract model (`reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/selectedRoute.ts`, `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add explicit helpers for orchestrated route requirements, for example `requiredChildRouteSpecs(parentTaskType, params)`, returning the child task types and route params that a parent may create.
2. Include `travel_orchestrator`, `join_clips_orchestrator`, and `edit_video_orchestrator` parent route keys in the selector maps as parent/control routes, while keeping actual child route support authoritative.
3. Add `isRouteSupportedForBackend(resolved)` / `assertBackendConsistent(parent, children)` style helpers in both TypeScript and Python so route support checks do not drift.
4. Treat `vibecomfy_unsupported`, `wgp_only`, unknown routes, missing template ids, and malformed snapshots as hard failures for a VibeComfy-selected parent.

### Step 2: Make parent create-time validation fail closed (`reigh-app/supabase/functions/create-task/routeContract.ts`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/create-task/index.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Preserve the current stamping path, but add a validation step after resolver output and before the insert loop.
2. For orchestrated parent task types, inspect the stamped parent `route_contract`; when `selected_backend === "vibecomfy"`, derive all required child route contracts and reject the request if any child is not `vibecomfy_supported`.
3. Return a 400 `validation_error` or route-specific error code before any task insert happens.
4. Add tests showing no partial parent task is inserted when a VibeComfy parent would require WGP-only or unsupported children.

## Phase 2: Child Route Propagation — Worker-Created Rows

### Step 3: Centralize child route snapshot building (`reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py`, new small helper if needed)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a helper such as `child_route_snapshot_from_parent(parent_params, child_task_type, child_params, parent_task_type, child_task_id=None)`.
2. Copy parent selector namespace, selected backend, selector version, selected profile, route run id, and worker contract version from `parent_params.route_contract`.
3. Derive the child route key/support state from child params and add `parent_route_key` to the nested snapshot.
4. Fail closed if the child route support state cannot satisfy the parent selected backend.

### Step 4: Wire travel child creation (`reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/travel/orchestrator.py`, `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/travel/orchestration/orchestrator.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Find every `add_task_to_db()` call that creates `travel_segment`, `individual_travel_segment`, `travel_stitch`, or nested join parents from travel orchestration.
2. Replace ad hoc or missing route metadata with the centralized child snapshot helper.
3. Preserve existing `dependant_on` arrays, run ids, `orchestrator_task_id_ref`, `segment_index`, parent generation ids, and idempotency checks.
4. Add an early preflight before the first child insert so a VibeComfy-selected travel parent rejects if any planned segment/stitch/join route is unsupported.

### Step 5: Wire join and edit-video child creation (`reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/join/task_builder.py`, `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/join/orchestrator.py`, `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/edit_video_orchestrator.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Replace the hard-coded `backend="wgp"` snapshots in join transition creation with parent-derived snapshots.
2. Keep final local stitch rows as WGP/local-only unless they become selected routes; document that choice in tests by asserting their route contract behavior.
3. Ensure edit-video uses the same helper through `create_join_chain_tasks()` and records `[REDACTED]`, not `join_clips_orchestrator`, when the join chain was spawned by edit-video.
4. Preserve chain dependencies and parallel `dependant_on` arrays exactly as today.

## Phase 3: Lifecycle And Repair Coverage

### Step 6: Strengthen lifecycle checks (`reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/join/shared.py`, travel idempotency helpers, complete-task functions)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend existing idempotency checks to detect mixed-backend child sets by reading `params.route_contract.selected_backend` across children.
2. Return a terminal failure or repair-required orchestration result when existing children for one parent have inconsistent selected backends or selector versions.
3. Keep duplicate completion handling in `reigh-app/supabase/functions/complete_task/generation-core.ts` and child generation paths unchanged unless tests expose a route-specific hole.
4. Add tests for partial children, duplicate completion, already-uploaded output with missing completion, cancellation mid-spawn, and mixed backend children.

### Step 7: Add repair/runbook hooks (`reigh-worker/debug`, `reigh-worker-orchestrator/scripts/debug`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/get-orchestrator-children`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend orchestrator-child debug output to show `route_contract.route_key`, `selected_backend`, `selector_version`, `support_state`, and `parent_route_key`.
2. Add a focused query/helper for mixed-backend child sets and uploaded-but-not-completed outputs.
3. Keep repair hooks read/report first; only add mutation actions if there is already an established repair command pattern.

## Phase 4: Tests And Validation

### Step 8: Add targeted tests first
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add TypeScript create-task tests in `reigh-app/supabase/functions/create-task/routeContract.test.ts` and resolver tests for travel/join/edit-video parent rejection.
2. Add Python routing tests in `reigh-worker/tests/test_template_routing.py` for parent-derived child snapshots and fail-closed VibeComfy parents.
3. Add worker lifecycle tests for dependency-array preservation, cancellation, duplicate child creation/idempotency, and mixed-backend detection.

### Step 9: Run validation in cheap-to-broad order
**Scope:** Small
1. Run focused Deno/Vitest tests for `create-task` route contract.
2. Run focused pytest files: `reigh-worker/tests/test_template_routing.py`, join orchestrator tests, edit-video orchestrator tests, and travel orchestrator lifecycle tests.
3. Run broader Supabase function tests and worker tests only after targeted checks are green.

## Execution Order
1. Add shared route requirement helpers and tests before changing orchestration behavior.
2. Enforce API parent validation before worker child propagation, so new parent rows cannot enter an impossible selected-backend state.
3. Wire child propagation one surface at a time: join first, edit-video second, travel last because travel can spawn multiple child families.
4. Add repair/debug visibility after route metadata is consistently persisted.

## Validation Order
1. `cd reigh-app && deno test supabase/functions/create-task/routeContract.test.ts supabase/functions/create-task/index.test.ts`
2. `cd reigh-worker && pytest tests/test_template_routing.py tests/test_join_orchestrator_and_registry.py tests/test_join_orchestrator_payload_contracts.py`
3. Add and run focused edit-video/travel lifecycle tests once the touched files are known.
4. Finish with the relevant Supabase function suite and worker pytest subset used by CI for task routing/lifecycle.

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-008",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-143",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-156",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the direct adapter still has a correctness risk around resolution parity. current vibecomfy ready templates hardcode image dimensions (`image/z_image` uses `emptysd3latentimage` 1024x1024 and `image/qwen_image_2512` uses 768x768), while `vibecomfy run` exposes no `--resolution` flag; the revised plan says a temporary scratchpad/input file may be generated only if supported, otherwise resolution is left out. that means a vibecomfy-supported direct route can silently ignore the worker's `resolution` param unless scratchpad resolution patching is made mandatory for supported direct routes or the support is explicitly limited to default-resolution smoke fixtures.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-108",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-145",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised v4 body against `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md` sprint 2. the repo plan doc still lists an \"ltx-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline\" as a sprint 2 verification gate and repeats that smoke in the functionality-preservation checks, but the revised execution steps no longer schedule that smoke anywhere; it survives only as an info-level metadata criterion. because `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` remains an explicit sprint 2 deliverable, the revised plan still only partially carries forward the verification package described in the source migration plan.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-153",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: worker integration: the migration doc describes the per-call profile override gap as involving reigh-worker adapter handling of override_profile before constructing vibecomfy sessionconfig, but the plan limits sprint 1 per-run work to the vibecomfy cli/runtime. that may be acceptable for a vibecomfy-library mvp, but it does not fully exercise the production-facing per-task override semantics called out in the overall context.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-vibecomfy-memory-20260506-0147"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-155",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: a residual issue-hint tension remains for resolution handling. the plan acknowledges current `vibecomfy run` lacks a resolution flag, but allows the adapter to leave resolution and other template-specific values unwired for sprint 2. because `z_image_turbo` and `qwen_image_2512` are marked vibecomfy-supported direct routes, this may not preserve the existing worker task-parameter contract for tasks that provide a non-default `resolution`.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-110",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-142",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting-infrastructure review still finds one missing check. in `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md`, sprint 2's verification matrix and risk register both single out `self_refiner` as a distinct drift-upgrade surface with its own smoke and its own silent-behavior-change risk, but the revised v4 body updates the file without keeping that supporting verification step in the actual execution checklist.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-154",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: documentation/data gap: the sprint exit criteria require profiles 1 and 3 to have vram and wall-clock data, but the plan puts that only in metadata as an info-level criterion and does not schedule a concrete artifact location or command for recording the measurements. the implementation could satisfy all unit tests while still missing one of the explicit exit criteria.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-vibecomfy-memory-20260506-0147"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-158",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting infrastructure for resolution is still incomplete if direct vibecomfy routes are considered production-supported. vibecomfy has a `vibecomfy.patches.resolution` helper and the cli can load scratchpad files with `allow_scratchpad=true`, but the plan does not require using that path; it permits leaving resolution out for sprint 2.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-162",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked vibecomfy profile wording against `vibecomfy/tests/test_ready_templates.py`. the revised plan says to add the production template to representative compile profile coverage 'alongside the existing dry-run id', but the dry-run id is not currently in `profile_smoke_template_ids`; only `video/wanvideo_wrapper_22_5b_i2v` and `video/wan_t2v` are. this is a small wording mismatch rather than a functional blocker because adding either the production id alone or both ids would still satisfy sprint 4.",

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-146",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: finding callers of the changed module shows that `shared.utils.self_refiner` is consumed by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py`, multiple ltx2 pipeline modules, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`. the revised validation steps still only drive the three bridge getters in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py`; none of the scheduled commands execute a real `self_refiner` caller, so the plan does not yet verify the main caller shapes for the other explicit sprint 2 file change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-159",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked direct queue caller values in `db_task_to_generation_task()`: direct image tasks may carry `resolution`, `steps`/`num_inference_steps`, `seed`, and `override_profile`. the revised adapter plan covers seed, steps, and memory profile but not guaranteed resolution propagation, so it does not handle all relevant direct-route caller arguments for a supported vibecomfy direct route.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    }
  ],

  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 7,
    "plan_count": 4,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-143",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-156",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the direct adapter still has a correctness risk around resolution parity. current vibecomfy ready templates hardcode image dimensions (`image/z_image` uses `emptysd3latentimage` 1024x1024 and `image/qwen_image_2512` uses 768x768), while `vibecomfy run` exposes no `--resolution` flag; the revised plan says a temporary scratchpad/input file may be generated only if supported, otherwise resolution is left out. that means a vibecomfy-supported direct route can silently ignore the worker's `resolution` param unless scratchpad resolution patching is made mandatory for supported direct routes or the support is explicitly limited to default-resolution smoke fixtures.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },

  {
    "subsystem": "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure",
    "total_occurrences": 6,
    "plan_count": 6,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-014",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-110",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-142",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting-infrastructure review still finds one missing check. in `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md`, sprint 2's verification matrix and risk register both single out `self_refiner` as a distinct drift-upgrade surface with its own smoke and its own silent-behavior-change risk, but the revised v4 body updates the file without keeping that supporting verification step in the actual execution checklist.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-154",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: documentation/data gap: the sprint exit criteria require profiles 1 and 3 to have vram and wall-clock data, but the plan puts that only in metadata as an info-level criterion and does not schedule a concrete artifact location or command for recording the measurements. the implementation could satisfy all unit tests while still missing one of the explicit exit criteria.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,

        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-158",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting infrastructure for resolution is still incomplete if direct vibecomfy routes are considered production-supported. vibecomfy has a `vibecomfy.patches.resolution` helper and the cli can load scratchpad files with `allow_scratchpad=true`, but the plan does not require using that path; it permits leaving resolution out for sprint 2.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,

        "id": "DEBT-162",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked vibecomfy profile wording against `vibecomfy/tests/test_ready_templates.py`. the revised plan says to add the production template to representative compile profile coverage 'alongside the existing dry-run id', but the dry-run id is not currently in `profile_smoke_template_ids`; only `video/wanvideo_wrapper_22_5b_i2v` and `video/wan_t2v` are. this is a small wording mismatch rather than a functional blocker because adding either the production id alone or both ids would still satisfy sprint 4.",

  {
    "subsystem": "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements",
    "total_occurrences": 5,
    "plan_count": 5,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-013",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-108",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-145",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised v4 body against `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md` sprint 2. the repo plan doc still lists an \"ltx-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline\" as a sprint 2 verification gate and repeats that smoke in the functionality-preservation checks, but the revised execution steps no longer schedule that smoke anywhere; it survives only as an info-level metadata criterion. because `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` remains an explicit sprint 2 deliverable, the revised plan still only partially carries forward the verification package described in the source migration plan.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-153",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: worker integration: the migration doc describes the per-call profile override gap as involving reigh-worker adapter handling of override_profile before constructing vibecomfy sessionconfig, but the plan limits sprint 1 per-run work to the vibecomfy cli/runtime. that may be acceptable for a vibecomfy-library mvp, but it does not fully exercise the production-facing per-task override semantics called out in the overall context.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-1-vibecomfy-memory-20260506-0147"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-155",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: a residual issue-hint tension remains for resolution handling. the plan acknowledges current `vibecomfy run` lacks a resolution flag, but allows the adapter to leave resolution and other template-specific values unwired for sprint 2. because `z_image_turbo` and `qwen_image_2512` are marked vibecomfy-supported direct routes, this may not preserve the existing worker task-parameter contract for tasks that provide a non-default `resolution`.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
        ]
      }

        "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

        Idea:
# Sprint 8: Orchestrated Route Propagation and Lifecycle Contract

## Overall Context

This sprint brings parent/child workflows onto the selector contract. It is the point where travel, join, and edit-video orchestration must reject mixed or unsupported backend combinations before creating partial work.

## Shared Operating Rules

- Parent backend selection is authoritative only when all required child routes are supported, tested, and not WGP-only.
- Child rows must carry route snapshot metadata: selected backend, selector version, parent route key, and support state.
- Preserve dependency arrays, idempotency, cancellation, duplicate completion handling, and repair paths.
- Fail closed before child creation when backend consistency cannot be guaranteed.

## Sprint Goal

Route parent/child workflows consistently through selected backend where Comfy support exists.

## Required Deliverables

- Propagation for travel/join/edit-video parent and child surfaces.
- Persisted child-row route snapshot.
- Dependency-array/idempotency/cancellation behavior for DB-created child rows.
- Parent/child backend-consistency guards.
- Lifecycle-contract tests.
- Repair/runbook hooks for partial children, uploaded-but-not-completed outputs, duplicate completion, mixed-backend child sets, and parent repair.

## Exit Criteria

Parent rejects Comfy if any required child route is WGP-only, unsupported, fallback, or untested; child rows created after parent claim carry enough metadata to avoid selector drift; lifecycle contract is green for every USED route intended for canary.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Sprint 8 Route Propagation And Lifecycle Contract

## Overview
The critique points to plan gaps, not a wrong root cause. The right root remains the route contract boundary between `reigh-app` task creation and `reigh-worker` child creation, but the plan must be stricter about the authoritative selection source and every child/control row that participates in orchestration.

This revision makes `params.route_contract` the sole persisted route snapshot source of truth. Create-time parent selection is explicit: `create-task` may accept a route selection candidate for the parent request; if absent, the existing WGP default remains. That keeps the normal WGP path stable while making VibeComfy canary selection reachable and testable. Parent VibeComfy selection must be rejected before any insert when required child/control rows cannot satisfy the selected backend. Worker child creation must then copy the claimed parent route contract, not ambient defaults, into every DB-created child/control row unless the row is explicitly documented and tested as a local terminal artifact.

## Phase 1: Selection Source And Contract Semantics

### Step 1: Define the authoritative create-time route selection input (`reigh-app/supabase/functions/create-task/request.ts`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/create-task/index.ts`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/create-task/routeContract.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add an optional top-level route selection candidate to the create-task request, reusing the existing route contract shape where possible: selected backend, selector namespace, selector version, selected profile, route run id, and worker contract version.
2. Apply that candidate to every resolver-produced parent task before `stampTaskRouteContract()` runs, instead of relying on resolver params to carry ad hoc legacy route fields.
3. Keep the current behavior when no route candidate is provided: `stampTaskRouteContract()` derives a WGP production/default contract.
4. Reject malformed route candidates with a 400 before any task insert, rather than silently defaulting to WGP for explicit canary requests.
5. Add tests proving VibeComfy parent selection is reachable through the new request field and absence of the field still stamps WGP.

### Step 2: Add shared orchestrated-route requirement helpers (`reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/selectedRoute.ts`, `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add selector entries for parent/control task types used in this sprint: `travel_orchestrator`, `join_clips_orchestrator`, `edit_video_orchestrator`, `travel_stitch`, `join_final_stitch`, and nested join parent rows created by travel.
2. Add `requiredChildRouteSpecs(parentTaskType, parentParams)` in TypeScript for create-time validation. It must classify all rows the parent can create: travel segments, travel stitch, nested `join_clips_orchestrator`, join transitions, join final stitch, and edit-video join transitions/final stitch.
3. Add the matching Python helpers for runtime child propagation and preflight.
4. Treat these support states as VibeComfy parent blockers: `wgp_only`, `vibecomfy_unsupported`, unknown route, missing template id, malformed route snapshot, fallback, and untested route.
5. Keep child/control rows valid for WGP-selected parents by stamping them with route contracts instead of leaving them unclassified.

### Step 3: Enforce parent fail-closed validation before inserts (`reigh-app/supabase/functions/create-task/index.ts`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/create-task/routeContract.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. After resolver output and route stamping, validate every orchestrated parent task before the insert loop begins.
2. If a stamped parent has `selected_backend: "vibecomfy"`, derive all required child/control route specs and reject if any cannot be VibeComfy-supported and tested.
3. Ensure multi-task resolver output remains all-or-nothing: any parent route validation failure returns a response before inserting the first task.
4. Add tests for travel, join, and edit-video VibeComfy rejection, including an assertion that the mocked task insert was never called.

## Phase 2: Worker Child Propagation

### Step 4: Build parent-derived child snapshots in one helper (`reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add `child_route_snapshot_from_parent(parent_params, child_task_type, child_params, parent_task_type, child_task_id=None, parent_route_key=None)`.
2. Read parent route data only from `parent_params.route_contract`; do not read environment defaults for selected backend, selector namespace, selector version, profile, route run id, or worker contract version.
3. Derive the child route key and support state from child task type and child params, then write `parent_route_key` into the nested `route_selection_snapshot`.
4. Fail closed if the parent route contract is missing/malformed or if the child/control route cannot satisfy the parent selected backend.
5. Add a companion preflight helper that evaluates a list of planned child/control rows before any `add_task_to_db()` call.

### Step 5: Replace all ambient `route_snapshot_fields()` call sites (`reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/join/task_builder.py`, travel child creation files)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Replace hard-coded `backend="wgp"`, `selector_namespace="production"`, and hard-coded parent route keys with the parent-derived helper.
2. Cover both join builders: `_create_join_chain_tasks()` and `_create_parallel_join_tasks()`.
3. Cover travel segment creation and any travel-created `travel_stitch` or nested `join_clips_orchestrator` rows that currently pass `route_snapshot_fields=None`.
4. Keep direct calls to `route_snapshot_fields()` only for true direct task selection where there is no claimed parent route contract.
5. Add tests that fail if hard-coded ambient propagation remains in child creation paths.

### Step 6: Thread spawning parent identity through join builders (`reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/join/task_builder.py`, `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/join/orchestrator.py`, `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/edit_video_orchestrator.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Change `create_join_chain_tasks()` and `create_parallel_join_tasks()` signatures to accept `parent_task_type` or `parent_route_key`, defaulting to `join_clips_orchestrator` only for direct join orchestration callers.
2. Pass `[REDACTED]` from `edit_video_orchestrator` when it reuses the join chain builder.
3. Pass `[REDACTED]` from direct join orchestration.
4. When travel creates a nested join parent, stamp that nested parent from the travel parent; later join children should derive from the nested join parent contract.
5. Add tests proving edit-video-spawned join children persist `[REDACTED]` and direct join children persist `[REDACTED]`.

### Step 7: Classify and stamp control child rows (`reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/travel/orchestrator.py`, join task builders, route selector files)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Treat `travel_stitch`, nested `join_clips_orchestrator`, and `join_final_stitch` as DB-created child/control rows that need `params.route_contract` unless implementation evidence shows a row is not inserted into `tasks`.
2. Add selector entries for these control rows with explicit support state. Initially classify local/WGP-only rows as `wgp_only`, making them VibeComfy blockers at parent validation time.
3. For WGP parents, stamp these rows with parent-derived backend/profile/selector fields and their own route key/support state.
4. Preserve existing `dependant_on` behavior: chain final rows depend on the prior join, and parallel final stitch depends on all transition ids.
5. Add tests for route snapshots on `travel_stitch`, nested join parent rows, and `join_final_stitch`, or tests documenting a deliberate exclusion if a row is not a DB task.

## Phase 3: Lifecycle, Debug, And Repair

### Step 8: Enforce mixed-contract lifecycle guards (`reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/join/shared.py`, travel idempotency helpers)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend existing child idempotency checks to read `params.route_contract` from child rows and compare selected backend, selector namespace, selector version, selected profile, and parent route key.
2. Return a terminal failure or explicit repair-required orchestration result when children under one parent have mixed selected backends, mixed selector versions, or wrong parent route keys.
3. Keep duplicate completion behavior in `reigh-app/supabase/functions/complete_task/generation-core.ts` and child generation handlers unchanged unless focused tests expose route-specific duplication bugs.
4. Add tests for partial children, duplicate re-entry, uploaded output with incomplete status, cancellation mid-spawn, and mixed-backend/mixed-selector child sets.

### Step 9: Make nested route contracts visible to repair/debug paths (`reigh-app/supabase/functions/get-orchestrator-children/index.ts`, `reigh-worker/debug`, `reigh-worker-orchestrator/scripts/debug`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Keep `params.route_contract` as the source of truth; do not introduce top-level task route columns in this sprint.
2. Confirm `get-orchestrator-children` returns `params` for every child and add tests that route contracts are available through that existing response shape.
3. Extend debug formatters to display nested route contract fields: route key, selected backend, selector version, support state, selected profile, and parent route key.
4. Add read-only repair diagnostics for partial children, uploaded-but-not-completed outputs, duplicate completion candidates, mixed-backend child sets, and parent repair candidates.

## Phase 4: Tests And Validation

### Step 10: Add focused contract tests before broad suites
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add TypeScript tests for request-level route selection, VibeComfy parent rejection, WGP default behavior, and all-or-nothing task insertion.
2. Add Python tests in `reigh-worker/tests/test_template_routing.py` for parent-derived child snapshots, malformed parent contract failure, control-row classification, and replacement of ambient hard-coded route context.
3. Add join/edit-video tests proving the new parent route key parameter is passed correctly.
4. Add travel tests for segment, stitch, and nested join parent route snapshots.
5. Add lifecycle tests for dependency-array preservation, cancellation, idempotency, duplicate completion, uploaded-but-not-completed reporting, and mixed-backend detection.

### Step 11: Run validation in cheap-to-broad order
**Scope:** Small
1. Run focused create-task tests first.
2. Run focused worker route and join/edit-video tests next.
3. Run focused travel lifecycle tests after travel row stamping is wired.
4. Finish with the relevant Supabase function test subset and worker pytest routing/lifecycle subset used by CI.

## Execution Order
1. Define explicit create-time route selection and tests before adding fail-closed parent validation.
2. Add shared route requirement helpers and selector entries before worker child propagation.
3. Enforce API all-or-nothing parent validation before changing worker child creation.
4. Add the parent-derived Python helper, then replace every ambient child snapshot call site.
5. Thread parent route identity through join builders before relying on them for edit-video tests.
6. Stamp control child rows and lifecycle/debug paths after the route helper is stable.

## Validation Order
1. `cd reigh-app && deno test supabase/functions/create-task/routeContract.test.ts supabase/functions/create-task/index.test.ts`
2. `cd reigh-worker && pytest tests/test_template_routing.py tests/test_join_orchestrator_and_registry.py tests/test_join_orchestrator_payload_contracts.py`
3. Run the new focused edit-video and travel route lifecycle tests.
4. Run the broader Supabase function and worker routing/lifecycle subsets once focused tests are green.

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-008",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-143",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-156",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the direct adapter still has a correctness risk around resolution parity. current vibecomfy ready templates hardcode image dimensions (`image/z_image` uses `emptysd3latentimage` 1024x1024 and `image/qwen_image_2512` uses 768x768), while `vibecomfy run` exposes no `--resolution` flag; the revised plan says a temporary scratchpad/input file may be generated only if supported, otherwise resolution is left out. that means a vibecomfy-supported direct route can silently ignore the worker's `resolution` param unless scratchpad resolution patching is made mandatory for supported direct routes or the support is explicitly limited to default-resolution smoke fixtures.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable": [

  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-108",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-145",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised v4 body against `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md` sprint 2. the repo plan doc still lists an \"ltx-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline\" as a sprint 2 verification gate and repeats that smoke in the functionality-preservation checks, but the revised execution steps no longer schedule that smoke anywhere; it survives only as an info-level metadata criterion. because `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` remains an explicit sprint 2 deliverable, the revised plan still only partially carries forward the verification package described in the source migration plan.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-153",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: worker integration: the migration doc describes the per-call profile override gap as involving reigh-worker adapter handling of override_profile before constructing vibecomfy sessionconfig, but the plan limits sprint 1 per-run work to the vibecomfy cli/runtime. that may be acceptable for a vibecomfy-library mvp, but it does not fully exercise the production-facing per-task override semantics called out in the overall context.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-vibecomfy-memory-20260506-0147"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-155",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: a residual issue-hint tension remains for resolution handling. the plan acknowledges current `vibecomfy run` lacks a resolution flag, but allows the adapter to leave resolution and other template-specific values unwired for sprint 2. because `z_image_turbo` and `qwen_image_2512` are marked vibecomfy-supported direct routes, this may not preserve the existing worker task-parameter contract for tasks that provide a non-default `resolution`.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-110",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-142",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting-infrastructure review still finds one missing check. in `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md`, sprint 2's verification matrix and risk register both single out `self_refiner` as a distinct drift-upgrade surface with its own smoke and its own silent-behavior-change risk, but the revised v4 body updates the file without keeping that supporting verification step in the actual execution checklist.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-154",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: documentation/data gap: the sprint exit criteria require profiles 1 and 3 to have vram and wall-clock data, but the plan puts that only in metadata as an info-level criterion and does not schedule a concrete artifact location or command for recording the measurements. the implementation could satisfy all unit tests while still missing one of the explicit exit criteria.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-vibecomfy-memory-20260506-0147"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-158",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting infrastructure for resolution is still incomplete if direct vibecomfy routes are considered production-supported. vibecomfy has a `vibecomfy.patches.resolution` helper and the cli can load scratchpad files with `allow_scratchpad=true`, but the plan does not require using that path; it permits leaving resolution out for sprint 2.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

      "id": "DEBT-162",

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-146",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: finding callers of the changed module shows that `shared.utils.self_refiner` is consumed by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py`, multiple ltx2 pipeline modules, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`. the revised validation steps still only drive the three bridge getters in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py`; none of the scheduled commands execute a real `self_refiner` caller, so the plan does not yet verify the main caller shapes for the other explicit sprint 2 file change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-159",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked direct queue caller values in `db_task_to_generation_task()`: direct image tasks may carry `resolution`, `steps`/`num_inference_steps`, `seed`, and `override_profile`. the revised adapter plan covers seed, steps, and memory profile but not guaranteed resolution propagation, so it does not handle all relevant direct-route caller arguments for a supported vibecomfy direct route.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    }
  ],
